sidebar:

import { Tabs, TabItem } from '@astrojs/starlight/components';

This is an interesting sutta that documents the Buddha's renunciation and search for liberation from suffering. It would seem to be a late text that serves the purpose of emphasising the Buddha's rejection of the practices of his teachers, and documents his own path.

The sutta is considered late because the accompanying discourse "Heap of traps" (`pāsarāsi`) contains a number of post-sectarian concepts, makes numerous references to Māra, and avoids mentioning any concepts from the early discourses:

* five objects of sensual desire (`kāmaguṇā`)
* four mundane jhānā
* four formless jhānā
* cessation of apperception and feeling processes (`saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ`)
* destruction of defilements (`āsavā`)

It is interesting that the sutta integrates the rejected practices (the bases of nothingness and neither apperception nor non-apperception) back into the path, which suggests the author was trying to reconcile the apparent contradiction of the stories of the Buddha's rejection of Āḷāra Kālāma and Uddaka son of Rāma's (which presumably predated this sutta) into a thinly disguised attempt to legitimise late practices such as the formless `jhānā` and the destruction of the defilements. The suggestion here is that the Buddha was able to transcend both practices into the "cessation of apperception and feeling processes" and thus destroy the defilements. This is of course contrary to the eightfold path which is an active process based on a reflective feedback loop.

## Introduction

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
933\. Thus heard by me —

At one time the Bhagavā was dwelling at Sāvatthī, in Jeta's Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika's Park. Then, in the morning, having dressed and taking his bowl and robes, the Bhagavā entered Sāvatthī for alms. Then several bhikkhū went to where āyasmā Ānanda was; having approached, they said this to āyasmā Ānanda:

> “It has been a long time, Āvuso Ānanda, since we heard a Dhamma talk from the Bhagavā’s own mouth. It would be good, Āvuso Ānanda, if we could hear a Dhamma talk from the Bhagavā’s own mouth.”

> “Then, venerable ones, go to the hermitage of Rammaka the brahmin; perhaps you might get to hear a Dhamma talk from the Bhagavā’s own mouth.”

“Yes, Āvuso,” those bhikkhū replied to āyasmā Ānanda.

934\. Then, after wandering for alms in Sāvatthī and returning from his alms-round after the meal, the Bhagavā addressed āyasmā Ānanda:

> “Come, Ānanda, let us go to the Eastern Park, the Palace of Migāra’s Mother, for the day’s abiding.”

> “Yes, Bhante,” āyasmā Ānanda replied to the Bhagavā. Then the Bhagavā, together with āyasmā Ānanda, went to the Eastern Park, the Palace of Migāra’s Mother, for the day’s abiding. Then, in the evening, having risen from seclusion, the Bhagavā addressed āyasmā Ānanda:

> “Come, Ānanda, let us go to the Eastern Gatehouse to bathe our limbs.”

> “Yes, Bhante,” āyasmā Ānanda replied to the Bhagavā.

935\. Then the Bhagavā, together with āyasmā Ānanda, went to the Eastern Gatehouse to bathe his limbs. Having bathed his limbs at the Eastern Gatehouse, and having come out, he stood there, dressed in a single robe, drying his limbs. Then āyasmā Ānanda said this to the Bhagavā:

> “Bhante, the hermitage of Rammaka the brahmin is not far away. Delightful, Bhante, is the hermitage of Rammaka the brahmin; charming, Bhante, is the hermitage of Rammaka the brahmin. It would be good, Bhante, if the Bhagavā would go to the hermitage of Rammaka the brahmin, out of compassion.”

The Bhagavā consented by silence.

936\. Then the Bhagavā went to the hermitage of Rammaka the brahmin. Now at that time, several bhikkhū were sitting together in Rammaka the brahmin’s hermitage, engaged in a Dhamma talk. Then the Bhagavā stood outside the outer door, waiting for their talk to finish. Then, knowing that their talk had finished, the Bhagavā coughed and tapped on the door bar. Those bhikkhū opened the door for the Bhagavā. Then the Bhagavā entered Rammaka the brahmin’s hermitage and sat down on the prepared seat. Having sat down, the Bhagavā addressed the bhikkhū:

> “Bhikkhave, what talk were you engaged in just now, as you were sitting together? And what was the topic of conversation that was interrupted?”

> “Bhante, our Dhamma talk, which was indeed about the Bhagavā himself, was interrupted, and then the Bhagavā arrived.”

937\. “Excellent, Bhikkhave. It is indeed fitting for you, Bhikkhave, as young men of good families who have gone forth from the home life into homelessness out of faith, that you should sit together engaged in a Dhamma talk. When you are gathered together, Bhikkhave, one of two things should be done: either a Dhamma talk, or the silence of the liberated.”

938\. Bhikkhave, there are these two `pariyesanā` (quests): the quest of the liberated and the quest of the unliberated.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
933\. Evaṃ me sutaṃ—  ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho bhagavā pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Atha kho sambahulā bhikkhū yenāyasmā ānando tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ etadavocuṃ—  “cirassutā no, āvuso ānanda, bhagavato sammukhā dhammī kathā. Sādhu mayaṃ, āvuso ānanda, labheyyāma bhagavato sammukhā dhammiṃ kathaṃ savanāyā”ti. “Tena hāyasmanto yena rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamo tenupasaṅkamatha; appeva nāma labheyyātha bhagavato sammukhā dhammiṃ kathaṃ savanāyā”ti. “Evamāvuso”ti kho te bhikkhū āyasmato ānandassa paccassosuṃ.

934\. Atha kho bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi—  “āyāmānanda, yena pubbārāmo migāramātupāsādo tenupasaṅkamissāma divāvihārāyā”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paccassosi. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmatā ānandena saddhiṃ yena pubbārāmo migāramātupāsādo tenupasaṅkami divāvihārāya. Atha kho bhagavā sāyanhasamayaṃ paṭisallānā vuṭṭhito āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ āmantesi—  “āyāmānanda, yena pubbakoṭṭhako tenupasaṅkamissāma gattāni parisiñcitun”ti. “Evaṃ, bhante”ti kho āyasmā ānando bhagavato paccassosi.

935\. Atha kho bhagavā āyasmatā ānandena saddhiṃ yena pubbakoṭṭhako tenupasaṅkami gattāni parisiñcituṃ. Pubbakoṭṭhake gattāni parisiñcitvā paccuttaritvā ekacīvaro aṭṭhāsi gattāni pubbāpayamāno. Atha kho āyasmā ānando bhagavantaṃ etadavoca—  “ayaṃ, bhante, rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamo avidūre. Ramaṇīyo, bhante, rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamo; pāsādiko, bhante, rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamo. Sādhu, bhante, bhagavā yena rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamo tenupasaṅkamatu anukampaṃ upādāyā”ti. Adhivāsesi bhagavā tuṇhībhāvena.

936\. Atha kho bhagavā yena rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamo tenupasaṅkami. Tena kho pana samayena sambahulā bhikkhū rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assame dhammiyā kathāya sannisinnā honti. Atha kho bhagavā bahidvārakoṭṭhake aṭṭhāsi kathāpariyosānaṃ āgamayamāno. Atha kho bhagavā kathāpariyosānaṃ viditvā ukkāsitvā aggaḷaṃ ākoṭesi. Vivariṃsu kho te bhikkhū bhagavato dvāraṃ. Atha kho bhagavā rammakassa brāhmaṇassa assamaṃ pavisitvā paññatte āsane nisīdi. Nisajja kho bhagavā bhikkhū āmantesi—  “kāya nuttha, bhikkhave, etarahi kathāya sannisinnā? Kā ca pana vo antarākathā vippakatā”ti? “Bhagavantameva kho no, bhante, ārabbha dhammī kathā vippakatā, atha bhagavā anuppatto”ti.

937\. “Sādhu, bhikkhave. Etaṃ kho, bhikkhave, tumhākaṃ patirūpaṃ kulaputtānaṃ saddhā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajitānaṃ yaṃ tumhe dhammiyā kathāya sannisīdeyyātha. Sannipatitānaṃ vo, bhikkhave, dvayaṃ karaṇīyaṃ—  dhammī vā kathā, ariyo vā tuṇhībhāvo.

938\. Dvemā, bhikkhave, pariyesanā—  ariyā ca pariyesanā, anariyā ca pariyesanā.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
933\. 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀫𑁂 𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀁—  𑀏𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀺 𑀚𑁂𑀢𑀯𑀦𑁂 𑀅𑀦𑀸𑀣𑀧𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀺𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁂. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀲𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀘𑀻𑀯𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀲𑀺. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀳𑀼𑀮𑀸 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀽 𑀬𑁂𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁𑀲𑀼; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀼𑀁—  “𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤, 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀻 𑀓𑀣𑀸. 𑀲𑀸𑀥𑀼 𑀫𑀬𑀁, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤, 𑀮𑀪𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀫 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀁 𑀓𑀣𑀁 𑀲𑀯𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀸”𑀢𑀺. “𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀳𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀣; 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀯 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀮𑀪𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀣 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀁 𑀓𑀣𑀁 𑀲𑀯𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀸”𑀢𑀺. “𑀏𑀯𑀫𑀸𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄”𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀢𑁂 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀽 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀢𑁄 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁄𑀲𑀼𑀁.

934\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀸𑀬 𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀪𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀧𑀸𑀢𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀁 𑀆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀲𑀺—  “𑀆𑀬𑀸𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀫𑀺𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀢𑀼𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀤𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫 𑀤𑀺𑀯𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀬𑀸”𑀢𑀺. “𑀏𑀯𑀁, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂”𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁄𑀲𑀺. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀢𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀫𑀺𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀢𑀼𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀤𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺 𑀤𑀺𑀯𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀸𑀬. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀳𑀲𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀲𑀮𑁆𑀮𑀸𑀦𑀸 𑀯𑀼𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀁 𑀆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀲𑀺—  “𑀆𑀬𑀸𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀓𑁄𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀓𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫 𑀕𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀦𑁆”𑀢𑀺. “𑀏𑀯𑀁, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂”𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁄𑀲𑀺.

935\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀢𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀓𑁄𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀓𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺 𑀕𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀁. 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀓𑁄𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀓𑁂 𑀕𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀏𑀓𑀘𑀻𑀯𑀭𑁄 𑀅𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀕𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀧𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀆𑀦𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  “𑀅𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁄 𑀅𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀽𑀭𑁂. 𑀭𑀫𑀡𑀻𑀬𑁄, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁄; 𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀓𑁄, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁄. 𑀲𑀸𑀥𑀼, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀢𑀼 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀓𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀁 𑀉𑀧𑀸𑀤𑀸𑀬𑀸”𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀥𑀺𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁂𑀲𑀺 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀻𑀪𑀸𑀯𑁂𑀦.

936\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺. 𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀧𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀳𑀼𑀮𑀸 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀽 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑁂 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀓𑀣𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸 𑀳𑁄𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑀳𑀺𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀭𑀓𑁄𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀓𑁂 𑀅𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀓𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁄𑀲𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀆𑀕𑀫𑀬𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀓𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁄𑀲𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀍𑀅𑀁 𑀆𑀓𑁄𑀝𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀯𑀺𑀯𑀭𑀺𑀁𑀲𑀼 𑀔𑁄 𑀢𑁂 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀽 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑁄 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀭𑀁. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀭𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀸𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀡𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀯𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂 𑀆𑀲𑀦𑁂 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀺. 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀔𑁄 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀽 𑀆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀲𑀺—  “𑀓𑀸𑀬 𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀣, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀭𑀳𑀺 𑀓𑀣𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸? 𑀓𑀸 𑀘 𑀧𑀦 𑀯𑁄 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀸𑀓𑀣𑀸 𑀯𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀓𑀢𑀸”𑀢𑀺? “𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑁄 𑀦𑁄, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀆𑀭𑀩𑁆𑀪 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀻 𑀓𑀣𑀸 𑀯𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀓𑀢𑀸, 𑀅𑀣 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄”𑀢𑀺.

937\. “𑀲𑀸𑀥𑀼, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂. 𑀏𑀢𑀁 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀢𑀼𑀫𑁆𑀳𑀸𑀓𑀁 𑀧𑀢𑀺𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀁 𑀓𑀼𑀮𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸 𑀅𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑀺𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑀼𑀫𑁆𑀳𑁂 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀓𑀣𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀣. 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀺𑀧𑀢𑀺𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀯𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀬𑀁 𑀓𑀭𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀁—  𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀻 𑀯𑀸 𑀓𑀣𑀸, 𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁄 𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀻𑀪𑀸𑀯𑁄.

938\. 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑁂𑀫𑀸, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸—  𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀘 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸, 𑀅𑀦𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀘 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

## The Quest of the Unliberated

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
939\. And what, Bhikkhave, is the quest of the unliberated? Here, Bhikkhave, someone, being liable to birth from his own doing, searches only for what is liable to birth; being liable to old age from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to old age; being liable to sickness from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to sickness; being liable to death from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to death; being liable to sorrow from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to sorrow; being liable to defilement from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to defilement.

940\. And what, Bhikkhave, do you call liable to birth? Children and wives, Bhikkhave, are liable to birth; male and female slaves are liable to birth; goats and sheep are liable to birth; chickens and pigs are liable to birth; elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are liable to birth; gold and silver are liable to birth. For these, Bhikkhave, are acquisitions liable to birth. Entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in these, being liable to birth from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to birth.

941\. And what, Bhikkhave, do you call liable to old age? Children and wives, Bhikkhave, are liable to old age; male and female slaves are liable to old age; goats and sheep are liable to old age; chickens and pigs are liable to old age; elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are liable to old age; gold and silver are liable to old age. For these, Bhikkhave, are acquisitions liable to old age. Entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in these, being liable to old age from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to old age.

942\. And what, Bhikkhave, do you call liable to sickness? Children and wives, Bhikkhave, are liable to sickness; male and female slaves are liable to sickness; goats and sheep are liable to sickness; chickens and pigs are liable to sickness; elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are liable to sickness. For these, Bhikkhave, are acquisitions liable to sickness. Entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in these, being liable to sickness from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to sickness.

943\. And what, Bhikkhave, do you call liable to death? Children and wives, Bhikkhave, are liable to death; male and female slaves are liable to death; goats and sheep are liable to death; chickens and pigs are liable to death; elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are liable to death. For these, Bhikkhave, are acquisitions liable to death. Entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in these, being liable to death from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to death.

944\. And what, Bhikkhave, do you call liable to sorrow? Children and wives, Bhikkhave, are liable to sorrow; male and female slaves are liable to sorrow; goats and sheep are liable to sorrow; chickens and pigs are liable to sorrow; elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are liable to sorrow. For these, Bhikkhave, are acquisitions liable to sorrow. Entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in these, being liable to sorrow from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to sorrow.

945\. And what, Bhikkhave, do you call liable to defilement? Children and wives, Bhikkhave, are liable to defilement; male and female slaves are liable to defilement; goats and sheep are liable to defilement; chickens and pigs are liable to defilement; elephants, cattle, horses, and mares are liable to defilement; gold and silver are liable to defilement. For these, Bhikkhave, are acquisitions liable to defilement. Entangled, infatuated, and engrossed in these, being liable to defilement from his own doing, he searches only for what is liable to defilement. This, Bhikkhave, is the quest of the unliberated.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
939\. Katamā ca, bhikkhave, anariyā pariyesanā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhammaṃyeva pariyesati, attanā jarādhammo samāno jarādhammaṃyeva pariyesati, attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhammaṃyeva pariyesati, attanā maraṇadhammo samāno maraṇadhammaṃyeva pariyesati, attanā sokadhammo samāno sokadhammaṃyeva pariyesati, attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhammaṃyeva pariyesati.

940\. Kiñca, bhikkhave, jātidhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, jātidhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ jātidhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ jātidhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ jātidhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ jātidhammaṃ, jātarūparajataṃ jātidhammaṃ. Jātidhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo. Etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhammaṃyeva pariyesati.

941\. Kiñca, bhikkhave, jarādhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, jarādhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ jarādhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ jarādhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ jarādhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ jarādhammaṃ, jātarūparajataṃ jarādhammaṃ. Jarādhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo. Etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā jarādhammo samāno jarādhammaṃyeva pariyesati.

942\. Kiñca, bhikkhave, byādhidhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, byādhidhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ byādhidhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ byādhidhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ byādhidhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ byādhidhammaṃ. Byādhidhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo. Etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhammaṃyeva pariyesati.

943\. Kiñca, bhikkhave, maraṇadhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, maraṇadhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ maraṇadhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ maraṇadhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ maraṇadhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ maraṇadhammaṃ. Maraṇadhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo. Etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā maraṇadhammo samāno maraṇadhammaṃyeva pariyesati.

944\. Kiñca, bhikkhave, sokadhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, sokadhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ sokadhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ sokadhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ sokadhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ sokadhammaṃ. Sokadhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo. Etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā sokadhammo samāno sokadhammaṃyeva pariyesati.

945\. Kiñca, bhikkhave, saṃkilesadhammaṃ vadetha? Puttabhariyaṃ, bhikkhave, saṃkilesadhammaṃ, dāsidāsaṃ saṃkilesadhammaṃ, ajeḷakaṃ saṃkilesadhammaṃ, kukkuṭasūkaraṃ saṃkilesadhammaṃ, hatthigavāssavaḷavaṃ saṃkilesadhammaṃ, jātarūparajataṃ saṃkilesadhammaṃ. Saṃkilesadhammā hete, bhikkhave, upadhayo. Etthāyaṃ gathito mucchito ajjhāpanno attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhammaṃyeva pariyesati. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, anariyā pariyesanā.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
939\. 𑀓𑀢𑀫𑀸 𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀅𑀦𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸? 𑀇𑀥, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺.

940\. 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀣? 𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀚𑁂𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀓𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀼𑀝𑀲𑀽𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀕𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀍𑀅𑀯𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀭𑀚𑀢𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀧𑀥𑀬𑁄. 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺.

941\. 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀣? 𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀁 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀚𑁂𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀁 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀓𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀼𑀝𑀲𑀽𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀕𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀍𑀅𑀯𑀁 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀭𑀚𑀢𑀁 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀧𑀥𑀬𑁄. 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺.

942\. 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀣? 𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀁 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀚𑁂𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀁 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀓𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀼𑀝𑀲𑀽𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀕𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀍𑀅𑀯𑀁 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀧𑀥𑀬𑁄. 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺.

943\. 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀣? 𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀁 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀚𑁂𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀁 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀓𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀼𑀝𑀲𑀽𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀕𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀍𑀅𑀯𑀁 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀧𑀥𑀬𑁄. 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺.

944\. 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀣? 𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀁 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀚𑁂𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀓𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀼𑀝𑀲𑀽𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀕𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀍𑀅𑀯𑀁 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀧𑀥𑀬𑁄. 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺.

945\. 𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀣? 𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀪𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀲𑀁 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀚𑁂𑀍𑀅𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀓𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀼𑀝𑀲𑀽𑀓𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀳𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀕𑀯𑀸𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀍𑀅𑀯𑀁 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀭𑀽𑀧𑀭𑀚𑀢𑀁 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀳𑁂𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀧𑀥𑀬𑁄. 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀕𑀣𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀼𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀸𑀧𑀦𑁆𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀅𑀦𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

## The Quest of the Liberated

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
  946\. And what, Bhikkhave, is the quest of the liberated? Here, Bhikkhave, someone, being liable to birth from his own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to birth, searches for the unsurpassed sanctuary of `nibbāna` (extinguishment) from being born; being liable to old age from his own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to old age, searches for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-ageing through extinguishment; being liable to sickness from his own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sickness, searches for the unsurpassed sanctuary of health through extinguishment; being liable to death from his own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to death, searches for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-death through extinguishment; being liable to sorrow from his own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sorrow, searches for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-sorrow through extinguishment; being liable to defilement from his own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to defilement, searches for the unsurpassed sanctuary of undefilement through extinguishment. This, Bhikkhave, is the quest of the liberated.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
946\. Katamā ca, bhikkhave, ariyā pariyesanā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajātaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati, attanā jarādhammo samāno jarādhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajaraṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati, attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā abyādhiṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati, attanā maraṇadhammo samāno maraṇadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā amataṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati, attanā sokadhammo samāno sokadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asokaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati, attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asaṃkiliṭṭhaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesati. Ayaṃ, bhikkhave, ariyā pariyesanā.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
946\. 𑀓𑀢𑀫𑀸 𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸? 𑀇𑀥, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀫𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀬𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀅𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸.
</TabItem>

## Renunciation

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
  947\. Bhikkhave, I too, before my `sambodhi` (full awakening), while still a Bodhisatta, not fully awakened, being liable to birth from my own doing, searched only for what is liable to birth; being liable to old age from my own doing, I searched only for what is liable to old age; being liable to sickness from my own doing, I searched only for what is liable to sickness; being liable to death from my own doing, I searched only for what is liable to death; being liable to sorrow from my own doing, I searched only for what is liable to sorrow; being liable to defilement from my own doing, I searched only for what is liable to defilement. Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘Why do I, being liable to birth from my own doing, search only for what is liable to birth; being liable to old age from my own doing, search only for what is liable to old age; being liable to sickness from my own doing, search only for what is liable to sickness; being liable to death from my own doing, search only for what is liable to death; being liable to sorrow from my own doing, search only for what is liable to sorrow; being liable to defilement from my own doing, search only for what is liable to defilement? What if I, being liable to birth from my own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to birth, were to search for the unsurpassed sanctuary of extinguishment from being born; being liable to old age from my own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to old age, were to search for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-ageing through extinguishment; being liable to sickness from my own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sickness, were to search for the unsurpassed sanctuary of health through extinguishment; being liable to death from my own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to death, were to search for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-death through extinguishment; being liable to sorrow from my own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sorrow, were to search for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-sorrow through extinguishment; being liable to defilement from my own doing, having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to defilement, were to search for the unsurpassed sanctuary of undefilement through extinguishment?’
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
947\. Ahampi sudaṃ, bhikkhave, pubbeva sambodhā anabhisambuddho bodhisattova samāno attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi, attanā jarādhammo samāno jarādhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi, attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi, attanā maraṇadhammo samāno maraṇadhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi, attanā sokadhammo samāno sokadhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi, attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘kiṃ nu kho ahaṃ attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi, attanā jarādhammo samāno…pe…  byādhidhammo samāno…  maraṇadhammo samāno…  sokadhammo samāno…  attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhammaṃyeva pariyesāmi? Yannūnāhaṃ attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajātaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyeseyyaṃ, attanā jarādhammo samāno jarādhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajaraṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyeseyyaṃ, attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā abyādhiṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyeseyyaṃ, attanā maraṇadhammo samāno maraṇadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā amataṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyeseyyaṃ, attanā sokadhammo samāno sokadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asokaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyeseyyaṃ, attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asaṃkiliṭṭhaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyeseyyan’ti.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
947\. 𑀅𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀲𑀼𑀤𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀪𑀺𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑁄 𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀺𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄𑀯 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀓𑀺𑀁 𑀦𑀼 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄…𑀧𑁂…  𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄…  𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄…  𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄…  𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁𑀬𑁂𑀯 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀺? 𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀽𑀦𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀫𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

## Āḷāra Kālāma

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
948\. So, Bhikkhave, at a later time, while I was still young, with jet-black hair, endowed with the prime of youth, in the first stage of life—though my unwilling mother and father wept with tear-filled faces—having shaved off my hair and beard, and having put on saffron robes, I went forth from the home life into homelessness. Having thus gone forth, seeking what is wholesome, searching for the unsurpassed state of supreme peace, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was. Having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma: “I wish, Āvuso Kālāma, to lead the `brahmacariya` (pure life) in this Dhamma and Discipline.” When this was said, Bhikkhave, Āḷāra Kālāma said this to me: “May the venerable one stay; such is this Dhamma that a wise person, in no long time, having realised his own teacher's doctrine for himself through `abhiññā` (experiential understanding), may enter and dwell in it.” So, Bhikkhave, quickly, in no long time, I learned that Dhamma. So, Bhikkhave, merely by lip-service, by mere verbal declaration, I would speak of the doctrine of knowledge and the doctrine of elders, and I would claim, ‘I know, I see,’ and so would others. Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘It is not by mere faith alone that Āḷāra Kālāma declares this Dhamma as something he enters and dwells in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding; surely Āḷāra Kālāma dwells in this Dhamma knowing and seeing it.’

949\. Then, Bhikkhave, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was; having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma: ‘To what extent, Āvuso Kālāma, do you declare this Dhamma as something you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding?’ When this was said, Bhikkhave, Āḷāra Kālāma declared the `ākiñcaññāyatana` (base of nothingness). Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘It is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `saddhā` (faith); I also have faith; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `vīriya` (energy); I also have energy; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `sati` (awareness); I also have awareness; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `samādhi` (focus); I also have focus; it is not only Āḷāra Kālāma who has `paññā` (wisdom); I also have wisdom. What if I were to strive for the realisation of that Dhamma which Āḷāra Kālāma declares he enters and dwells in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding?’ So, Bhikkhave, quickly, in no long time, having realised that Dhamma for myself through experiential understanding, I entered and dwelt in it.

950\. Then, Bhikkhave, I approached where Āḷāra Kālāma was; having approached, I said this to Āḷāra Kālāma:

951\. ‘Is it to this extent, Āvuso Kālāma, that you declare this Dhamma, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding and entered upon it?’

952\. ‘To this extent indeed, Āvuso, do I declare this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it.’

953\. ‘Indeed I too, Āvuso, to this extent enter and dwell in this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding.’

954\. ‘It is a gain for us, Āvuso, it is a great gain for us, that we see such a venerable one as a companion in the pure life. Thus, the Dhamma that I declare, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it, that Dhamma you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding. The Dhamma that you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding, that Dhamma I declare, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding and entered upon it. Thus, the Dhamma that I know, that Dhamma you know; the Dhamma that you know, that Dhamma I know. Thus, as I am, so are you; as you are, so am I. Come now, Āvuso, let us both, being present, lead this company.’

955\. Thus, Bhikkhave, Āḷāra Kālāma, being my teacher, placed me, his own pupil, on the same level as himself, and honoured me with great honour. Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘This Dhamma does not lead to giving up, nor to dispassion, nor to cessation, nor to calm, nor to experiential understanding, nor to full awakening, nor to extinguishment; it leads only to reappearance in the base of nothingness.’ So, Bhikkhave, not valuing that Dhamma, having given up that Dhamma, I departed.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
948\. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, aparena samayena daharova samāno susukāḷakeso, bhadrena yobbanena samannāgato paṭhamena vayasā akāmakānaṃ mātāpitūnaṃ assumukhānaṃ rudantānaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajiṃ. So evaṃ pabbajito samāno kiṃkusalagavesī anuttaraṃ santivarapadaṃ pariyesamāno yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ. Upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘icchāmahaṃ, āvuso kālāma, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye brahmacariyaṃ caritun’ti. Evaṃ vutte, bhikkhave, āḷāro kālāmo maṃ etadavoca—  ‘viharatāyasmā; tādiso ayaṃ dhammo yattha viññū puriso nacirasseva sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihareyyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇiṃ. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, tāvatakeneva oṭṭhapahatamattena lapitalāpanamattena ñāṇavādañca vadāmi theravādañca, ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti ca paṭijānāmi ahañceva aññe ca. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘na kho āḷāro kālāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ kevalaṃ saddhāmattakena sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedeti; addhā āḷāro kālāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ jānaṃ passaṃ viharatī’ti.

949\. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘kittāvatā no, āvuso kālāma, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedesī’ti? Evaṃ vutte, bhikkhave, āḷāro kālāmo ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ pavedesi. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi saddhā, mayhampatthi saddhā; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi vīriyaṃ, mayhampatthi vīriyaṃ; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi sati, mayhampatthi sati; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi samādhi, mayhampatthi samādhi; na kho āḷārasseva kālāmassa atthi paññā, mayhampatthi paññā. Yannūnāhaṃ yaṃ dhammaṃ āḷāro kālāmo sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedeti, tassa dhammassa sacchikiriyāya padaheyyan’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsiṃ.

950\. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, yena āḷāro kālāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā āḷāraṃ kālāmaṃ etadavocaṃ—

951\. ‘Ettāvatā no, āvuso kālāma, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesī’ti?

952\. ‘Ettāvatā kho ahaṃ, āvuso, imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemī’ti.

953\. ‘Ahampi kho, āvuso, ettāvatā imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmī’ti.

954\. ‘Lābhā no, āvuso, suladdhaṃ no, āvuso, ye mayaṃ āyasmantaṃ tādisaṃ sabrahmacāriṃ passāma. Iti yāhaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi. Yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi tamahaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedemi. Iti yāhaṃ dhammaṃ jānāmi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi, yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi tamahaṃ dhammaṃ jānāmi. Iti yādiso ahaṃ tādiso tuvaṃ, yādiso tuvaṃ tādiso ahaṃ. Ehi dāni, āvuso, ubhova santā imaṃ gaṇaṃ pariharāmā’ti.

955\. Iti kho, bhikkhave, āḷāro kālāmo ācariyo me samāno attano antevāsiṃ maṃ samānaṃ attanā samasamaṃ ṭhapesi, uḷārāya ca maṃ pūjāya pūjesi. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘nāyaṃ dhammo nibbidāya na virāgāya na nirodhāya na upasamāya na abhiññāya na sambodhāya na nibbānāya saṃvattati, yāvadeva ākiñcaññāyatanūpapattiyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, taṃ dhammaṃ analaṅkaritvā tasmā dhammā nibbijja apakkamiṃ.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
948\. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀅𑀧𑀭𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀤𑀳𑀭𑁄𑀯 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀼𑀲𑀼𑀓𑀸𑀍𑀅𑀓𑁂𑀲𑁄, 𑀪𑀤𑁆𑀭𑁂𑀦 𑀬𑁄𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀦𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀸𑀕𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀞𑀫𑁂𑀦 𑀯𑀬𑀲𑀸 𑀅𑀓𑀸𑀫𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀫𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀧𑀺𑀢𑀽𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀫𑀼𑀔𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀭𑀼𑀤𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀓𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀁 𑀑𑀳𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀬𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀅𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀤𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀕𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑀺𑀁. 𑀲𑁄 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀚𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀓𑀺𑀁𑀓𑀼𑀲𑀮𑀕𑀯𑁂𑀲𑀻 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀯𑀭𑀧𑀤𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁. 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀇𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀫𑀳𑀁, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫, 𑀇𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀯𑀺𑀦𑀬𑁂 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  ‘𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸; 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀬𑀢𑁆𑀣 𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀽 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑀓𑀁 𑀆𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀧𑀼𑀡𑀺𑀁. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦𑁂𑀯 𑀑𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀳𑀢𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀮𑀧𑀺𑀢𑀮𑀸𑀧𑀦𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀯𑀤𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀣𑁂𑀭𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀜𑁆𑀘, ‘𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺 𑀘 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀅𑀳𑀜𑁆𑀘𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂 𑀘. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀓𑁂𑀯𑀮𑀁 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀢𑀺; 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀻’𑀢𑀺.

949\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀓𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫, 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺? 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀆𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑀺; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸. 𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀽𑀦𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀢𑀺, 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀺𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀤𑀳𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁.

950\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀁 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—

951\. ‘𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫, 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺?

952\. ‘𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺.

953\. ‘𑀅𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀔𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺.

954\. ‘𑀮𑀸𑀪𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀲𑀼𑀮𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀁 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀬𑁂 𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑀁 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀫𑀺 𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀲𑀺. 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀲𑀺 𑀢𑀫𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀫𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀺, 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀢𑀫𑀳𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁, 𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁. 𑀏𑀳𑀺 𑀤𑀸𑀦𑀺, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀉𑀪𑁄𑀯 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀕𑀡𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀸’𑀢𑀺.

955\. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀆𑀍𑀆𑀭𑁄 𑀓𑀸𑀮𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀆𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁄 𑀫𑁂 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀯𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁 𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀫𑀲𑀫𑀁 𑀞𑀧𑁂𑀲𑀺, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀬 𑀘 𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀽𑀚𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀽𑀚𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀯𑀺𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀁𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀢𑀺, 𑀬𑀸𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀯 𑀆𑀓𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀽𑀧𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀮𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀭𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀺𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀅𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

## Uddaka son of Rāma

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
956\. So, Bhikkhave, seeking what is wholesome, searching for the unsurpassed state of supreme peace, I approached where Uddaka son of Rāma was. Having approached, I said this to Uddaka son of Rāma: “I wish, Āvuso, to lead the pure life in this Dhamma and Discipline.” When this was said, Bhikkhave, Uddaka son of Rāma said this to me: “May the venerable one stay; such is this Dhamma that a wise person, in no long time, having realised his own teacher's doctrine for himself through experiential understanding, may enter and dwell in it.” So, Bhikkhave, quickly, in no long time, I learned that Dhamma. So, Bhikkhave, merely by lip-service, by mere verbal declaration, I would speak of the doctrine of knowledge and the doctrine of elders, and I would claim, ‘I know, I see,’ and so would others. Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘It was not by mere faith alone that Rāma declared this Dhamma as something he entered and dwelt in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding; surely Rāma dwelt in this Dhamma knowing and seeing it.’

957\. Then, Bhikkhave, I approached where Uddaka son of Rāma was; having approached, I said this to Uddaka son of Rāma: ‘To what extent, Āvuso, did Rāma declare this Dhamma as something he entered and dwelt in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding?’ When this was said, Bhikkhave, Uddaka son of Rāma declared the `nevasaññānāsaññāyatana` (base of neither apperception nor non-apperception). Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘It was not only Rāma who had faith; I also have faith; it was not only Rāma who had energy; I also have energy; it was not only Rāma who had awareness; I also have awareness; it was not only Rāma who had focus; I also have focus; it was not only Rāma who had wisdom; I also have wisdom. What if I were to strive for the realisation of that Dhamma which Rāma declared he entered and dwelt in, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding?’ So, Bhikkhave, quickly, in no long time, having realised that Dhamma for myself through experiential understanding, I entered and dwelt in it.

958\. Then, Bhikkhave, I approached where Uddaka son of Rāma was; having approached, I said this to Uddaka son of Rāma:

959\. ‘Was it to this extent, Āvuso, that Rāma declared this Dhamma, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding and entered upon it?’

960\. ‘To this extent indeed, Āvuso, did Rāma declare this Dhamma, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding and entered upon it.’

961\. ‘Indeed I too, Āvuso, to this extent enter and dwell in this Dhamma, having realised it for myself through experiential understanding.’

962\. ‘It is a gain for us, Āvuso, it is a great gain for us, that we see such a venerable one as a companion in the pure life. Thus, the Dhamma that Rāma declared, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding and entered upon it, that Dhamma you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding. The Dhamma that you enter and dwell in, having realised it for yourself through experiential understanding, that Dhamma Rāma declared, having realised it for himself through experiential understanding and entered upon it. Thus, the Dhamma that Rāma experientially understood, that Dhamma you know; the Dhamma that you know, that Dhamma Rāma experientially understood. Thus, as Rāma was, so are you; as you are, so was Rāma. Come now, Āvuso, you lead this company.’

963\. Thus, Bhikkhave, Uddaka son of Rāma, being my companion in the pure life, placed me in the position of teacher, and honoured me with great honour. Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘This Dhamma does not lead to giving up, nor to dispassion, nor to cessation, nor to calm, nor to experiential understanding, nor to full awakening, nor to extinguishment; it leads only to reappearance in the base of neither apperception nor non-apperception.’ So, Bhikkhave, not valuing that Dhamma, having given up that Dhamma, I departed.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
956\. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, kiṃ kusalagavesī anuttaraṃ santivarapadaṃ pariyesamāno yena udako rāmaputto tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā udakaṃ rāmaputtaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘icchāmahaṃ, āvuso, imasmiṃ dhammavinaye brahmacariyaṃ caritun’ti. Evaṃ vutte, bhikkhave, udako rāmaputto maṃ etadavoca—  ‘viharatāyasmā; tādiso ayaṃ dhammo yattha viññū puriso nacirasseva sakaṃ ācariyakaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihareyyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇiṃ. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, tāvatakeneva oṭṭhapahatamattena lapitalāpanamattena ñāṇavādañca vadāmi theravādañca, ‘jānāmi passāmī’ti ca paṭijānāmi ahañceva aññe ca. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘na kho rāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ kevalaṃ saddhāmattakena sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedesi; addhā rāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ jānaṃ passaṃ vihāsī’ti.

957\. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, yena udako rāmaputto tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā udakaṃ rāmaputtaṃ etadavocaṃ—  ‘kittāvatā no, āvuso, rāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedesī’ti? Evaṃ vutte, bhikkhave, udako rāmaputto nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ pavedesi. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘na kho rāmasseva ahosi saddhā, mayhampatthi saddhā; na kho rāmasseva ahosi vīriyaṃ, mayhampatthi vīriyaṃ; na kho rāmasseva ahosi sati, mayhampatthi sati; na kho rāmasseva ahosi samādhi, mayhampatthi samādhi, na kho rāmasseva ahosi paññā, mayhampatthi paññā. Yannūnāhaṃ yaṃ dhammaṃ rāmo sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmīti pavedesi, tassa dhammassa sacchikiriyāya padaheyyan’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, nacirasseva khippameva taṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja vihāsiṃ.

958\. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, yena udako rāmaputto tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā udakaṃ rāmaputtaṃ etadavocaṃ—

959\. ‘Ettāvatā no, āvuso, rāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesī’ti?

960\. ‘Ettāvatā kho, āvuso, rāmo imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesī’ti.

961\. ‘Ahampi kho, āvuso, ettāvatā imaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharāmī’ti.

962\. ‘Lābhā no, āvuso, suladdhaṃ no, āvuso, ye mayaṃ āyasmantaṃ tādisaṃ sabrahmacāriṃ passāma. Iti yaṃ dhammaṃ rāmo sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesi, taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi. Yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharasi, taṃ dhammaṃ rāmo sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja pavedesi. Iti yaṃ dhammaṃ rāmo abhiññāsi taṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi, yaṃ tvaṃ dhammaṃ jānāsi, taṃ dhammaṃ rāmo abhiññāsi. Iti yādiso rāmo ahosi tādiso tuvaṃ, yādiso tuvaṃ tādiso rāmo ahosi. Ehi dāni, āvuso, tuvaṃ imaṃ gaṇaṃ pariharā’ti.

963\. Iti kho, bhikkhave, udako rāmaputto sabrahmacārī me samāno ācariyaṭṭhāne maṃ ṭhapesi, uḷārāya ca maṃ pūjāya pūjesi. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘nāyaṃ dhammo nibbidāya na virāgāya na nirodhāya na upasamāya na abhiññāya na sambodhāya na nibbānāya saṃvattati, yāvadeva nevasaññānāsaññāyatanūpapattiyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, taṃ dhammaṃ analaṅkaritvā tasmā dhammā nibbijja apakkamiṃ.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
  956\. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀓𑀺𑀁 𑀓𑀼𑀲𑀮𑀕𑀯𑁂𑀲𑀻 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀯𑀭𑀧𑀤𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀇𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀸𑀫𑀳𑀁, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀇𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀺𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀯𑀺𑀦𑀬𑁂 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  ‘𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀢𑀸𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸; 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀅𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀬𑀢𑁆𑀣 𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀽 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀲𑀓𑀁 𑀆𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀧𑀼𑀡𑀺𑀁. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦𑁂𑀯 𑀑𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀧𑀳𑀢𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀮𑀧𑀺𑀢𑀮𑀸𑀧𑀦𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀦 𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀯𑀤𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀣𑁂𑀭𑀯𑀸𑀤𑀜𑁆𑀘, ‘𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺 𑀘 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀫𑀺 𑀅𑀳𑀜𑁆𑀘𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀜𑁆𑀜𑁂 𑀘. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀓𑁂𑀯𑀮𑀁 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸𑀫𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀓𑁂𑀦 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺; 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺.
  
  957\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—  ‘𑀓𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺? 𑀏𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀦𑁂𑀯𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀸; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀁; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑀺, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑀺; 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀥𑀺, 𑀦 𑀔𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸, 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀧𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸. 𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀦𑀽𑀦𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻𑀢𑀺 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺, 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀺𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀤𑀳𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀦𑁆’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀦𑀘𑀺𑀭𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑀺𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁.
  
  958\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁; 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘𑀁—
  
  959\. ‘𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺?
  
  960\. ‘𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀔𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀻’𑀢𑀺.
  
  961\. ‘𑀅𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀔𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀏𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀻’𑀢𑀺.
  
  962\. ‘𑀮𑀸𑀪𑀸 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀲𑀼𑀮𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀁 𑀦𑁄, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀬𑁂 𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀆𑀬𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑀁 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀫. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺, 𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀲𑀺. 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀲𑀺, 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀬𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸 𑀲𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀺𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀧𑀯𑁂𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀲𑀺 𑀢𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀺, 𑀬𑀁 𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀚𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀺, 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀲𑀺. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁, 𑀬𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁 𑀢𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺. 𑀏𑀳𑀺 𑀤𑀸𑀦𑀺, 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀲𑁄, 𑀢𑀼𑀯𑀁 𑀇𑀫𑀁 𑀕𑀡𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀸’𑀢𑀺.
  
  963\. 𑀇𑀢𑀺 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁄 𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀻 𑀫𑁂 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀆𑀘𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀸𑀦𑁂 𑀫𑀁 𑀞𑀧𑁂𑀲𑀺, 𑀉𑀍𑀆𑀭𑀸𑀬 𑀘 𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀽𑀚𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀽𑀚𑁂𑀲𑀺. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀺𑀤𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀯𑀺𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀉𑀧𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑁄𑀥𑀸𑀬 𑀦 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀬 𑀲𑀁𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀢𑀺, 𑀬𑀸𑀯𑀤𑁂𑀯 𑀦𑁂𑀯𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬𑀢𑀦𑀽𑀧𑀧𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀢𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀮𑀗𑁆𑀓𑀭𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀺𑀚𑁆𑀚 𑀅𑀧𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀺𑀁.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

## Awakening

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
964\. So, Bhikkhave, I, seeking what is wholesome, searching for the unsurpassed state of supreme peace, wandering by stages through Magadha, arrived at Uruvelā, the army township. There I saw a delightful stretch of land, a beautiful grove, a clear-flowing river with pleasant banks, and a village for alms around it. Then, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me: ‘Indeed, delightful is this stretch of land, beautiful is this grove, clear-flowing is this river with pleasant banks, and a village for alms is around it. This is indeed suitable for a young man of good family intent on striving.’ So, Bhikkhave, I sat down right there, thinking, ‘This is enough for striving.’

965\. That indeed, Bhikkhave, from my own doing I was liable to birth, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to birth, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of extinguishment from being born, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of extinguishment from being born. From my own doing I was liable to old age, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to old age, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-ageing through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-ageing through extinguishment. From my own doing I was liable to sickness, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sickness, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of health through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of health through extinguishment. From my own doing I was liable to death, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to death, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-death through extinguishment. From my own doing I was liable to sorrow, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to sorrow, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of non-sorrow through extinguishment. From my own doing I was liable to defilement, and having perceived the unsatisfactoriness of being liable to defilement, searching for the unsurpassed sanctuary of undefilement through extinguishment, achieved the unsurpassed sanctuary of undefilement through extinguishment. And knowledge and insight arose in me: ‘Unshakeable is my liberation, this is the final birth (of suffering), there is now no more appearing again (of suffering).’
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
964\. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, kiṃ kusalagavesī anuttaraṃ santivarapadaṃ pariyesamāno magadhesu anupubbena cārikaṃ caramāno yena uruvelā senānigamo tadavasariṃ. Tatthaddasaṃ ramaṇīyaṃ bhūmibhāgaṃ, pāsādikañca vanasaṇḍaṃ, nadiñca sandantiṃ setakaṃ supatitthaṃ ramaṇīyaṃ, samantā ca gocaragāmaṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘ramaṇīyo vata bho bhūmibhāgo, pāsādiko ca vanasaṇḍo, nadī ca sandati setakā supatitthā ramaṇīyā, samantā ca gocaragāmo. Alaṃ vatidaṃ kulaputtassa padhānatthikassa padhānāyā’ti. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, tattheva nisīdiṃ—  alamidaṃ padhānāyāti.

965\. So kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, attanā jātidhammo samāno jātidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajātaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesamāno ajātaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ ajjhagamaṃ, attanā jarādhammo samāno jarādhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā ajaraṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesamāno ajaraṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ ajjhagamaṃ, attanā byādhidhammo samāno byādhidhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā abyādhiṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesamāno abyādhiṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ ajjhagamaṃ, attanā maraṇadhammo samāno maraṇadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā amataṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ ajjhagamaṃ, attanā sokadhammo samāno sokadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asokaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ ajjhagamaṃ, attanā saṃkilesadhammo samāno saṃkilesadhamme ādīnavaṃ viditvā asaṃkiliṭṭhaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ pariyesamāno asaṃkiliṭṭhaṃ anuttaraṃ yogakkhemaṃ nibbānaṃ ajjhagamaṃ. Ñāṇañca pana me dassanaṃ udapādi—  ‘akuppā me vimutti, ayamantimā jāti, natthi dāni punabbhavo’ti.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
964\. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀓𑀺𑀁 𑀓𑀼𑀲𑀮𑀕𑀯𑁂𑀲𑀻 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀯𑀭𑀧𑀤𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀕𑀥𑁂𑀲𑀼 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁂𑀦 𑀘𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀓𑀁 𑀘𑀭𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀬𑁂𑀦 𑀉𑀭𑀼𑀯𑁂𑀮𑀸 𑀲𑁂𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀺𑀕𑀫𑁄 𑀢𑀤𑀯𑀲𑀭𑀺𑀁. 𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀁 𑀭𑀫𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀁 𑀪𑀽𑀫𑀺𑀪𑀸𑀕𑀁, 𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀓𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀯𑀦𑀲𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀁, 𑀦𑀤𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀁 𑀲𑁂𑀢𑀓𑀁 𑀲𑀼𑀧𑀢𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀁 𑀭𑀫𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀁, 𑀲𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘 𑀕𑁄𑀘𑀭𑀕𑀸𑀫𑀁. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀭𑀫𑀡𑀻𑀬𑁄 𑀯𑀢 𑀪𑁄 𑀪𑀽𑀫𑀺𑀪𑀸𑀕𑁄, 𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀤𑀺𑀓𑁄 𑀘 𑀯𑀦𑀲𑀡𑁆𑀟𑁄, 𑀦𑀤𑀻 𑀘 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀤𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑁂𑀢𑀓𑀸 𑀲𑀼𑀧𑀢𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀸 𑀭𑀫𑀡𑀻𑀬𑀸, 𑀲𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀘 𑀕𑁄𑀘𑀭𑀕𑀸𑀫𑁄. 𑀅𑀮𑀁 𑀯𑀢𑀺𑀤𑀁 𑀓𑀼𑀮𑀧𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀧𑀥𑀸𑀦𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺𑀓𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀧𑀥𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁂𑀯 𑀦𑀺𑀲𑀻𑀤𑀺𑀁—  𑀅𑀮𑀫𑀺𑀤𑀁 𑀧𑀥𑀸𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀸𑀢𑀺.

965\. 𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀕𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀚𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀚𑀭𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀕𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀩𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀥𑀺𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀕𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀫𑀭𑀡𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀫𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀕𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀕𑀫𑀁, 𑀅𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀦𑀸 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑁂𑀲𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁂 𑀆𑀤𑀻𑀦𑀯𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂𑀲𑀫𑀸𑀦𑁄 𑀅𑀲𑀁𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀁 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀁 𑀬𑁄𑀕𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂𑀫𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑀕𑀫𑀁. 𑀜𑀸𑀡𑀜𑁆𑀘 𑀧𑀦 𑀫𑁂 𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦𑀁 𑀉𑀤𑀧𑀸𑀤𑀺—  ‘𑀅𑀓𑀼𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀸 𑀫𑁂 𑀯𑀺𑀫𑀼𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀺, 𑀅𑀬𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀫𑀸 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺, 𑀦𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀺 𑀤𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀼𑀦𑀩𑁆𑀪𑀯𑁄’𑀢𑀺.
</TabItem>
</Tabs>

## Decision to teach

<Tabs syncKey="paliquote">
<TabItem label="My Translation">
966\. So, Bhikkhave, this thought occurred to me— ‘This Dhamma I have realised is indeed profound, hard to see, hard to comprehend, peaceful, refined, beyond the scope of reasoning, subtle, to be experienced by the wise. But this generation delights in attachment, is pleased by attachment, rejoices in attachment. For a generation delighting in attachment, pleased by attachment, rejoicing in attachment, this state is hard to see, namely: specific causality, `paṭiccasamuppāda` (dependent origination). This state, too, is indeed hard to see, namely: the calming of all `saṅkhārā` (mental constructions), the relinquishing of all acquisitions, the destruction of craving, dispassion, cessation, extinguishment. But if I were to teach the Dhamma and others did not understand me, that would be weariness for me, that would be trouble for me.’ And then, Bhikkhave, these unheard-before, wonderful verses occurred to me—

967\. ‘With difficulty have I realised it, \
enough now with proclaiming it; \
for those consumed by lust and hatred, \
this Dhamma is not easily understood. \
968\. Going against the stream, subtle, \
profound, hard to see, minute; \
those inflamed by lust will not see it, \
shrouded as they are in darkness.’

969\. So, Bhikkhave, as I reflected thus, my mind inclined to inaction, not to teaching the Dhamma. Then, Bhikkhave, Brahmā Sahampati, knowing my thought process with his own mind, had this thought— ‘Alas, the world is perishing, alas, the world is being destroyed, inasmuch as the mind of the Tathāgata, the `arahant` (liberated one), the `sammāsambuddha` (perfectly awakened one), inclines to inaction, not to teaching the Dhamma!’ Then, Bhikkhave, Brahmā Sahampati—just as a strong man might extend his flexed arm or flex his extended arm—even so, having vanished from the Brahma-world, appeared before me. Then, Bhikkhave, Brahmā Sahampati, arranging his upper robe over one shoulder, and having extended his clasped hands towards me, said this to me— ‘Bhante, may the Bhagavā teach the Dhamma, may the Fortunate One teach the Dhamma. There are beings with little dust in their eyes; they are perishing through not hearing the Dhamma. There will be those who understand the Dhamma.’ This Brahmā Sahampati said, Bhikkhave. Having said this, he then said further—

970\. ‘In Magadha there arose before, \
an impure Dhamma, conceived by those stained; \
open this gate to the deathless, \
let them hear the Dhamma, realised by the Stainless One. \
971\. Just as one standing on a mountain peak, \
might see the people all around; \
similarly, O wise one, O all-seeing one, \
having ascended the Dhamma-pavilion; \
look down upon the people immersed in sorrow, free from sorrow, \
look upon the people overcome by birth and ageing. \
972\. Arise, hero, victor in battle, \
caravan-leader, debtless one, wander in the world; \
may the Bhagavā teach the Dhamma, \
there will be those who understand it.’

973\. Then, Bhikkhave, knowing the request of Brahmā and out of compassion for beings, I surveyed the world with the Buddha-eye. As I surveyed the world with the Buddha-eye, I saw, Bhikkhave, beings with little dust in their eyes and with much dust in their eyes, with keen faculties and with dull faculties, with good dispositions and with bad dispositions, easy to teach and hard to teach, some who dwelt seeing fear in the blameworthy actions of the next world, and some who did not dwell seeing fear in the blameworthy actions of the next world. Just as in a pond of blue lotuses, or red lotuses, or white lotuses, some blue lotuses, red lotuses, or white lotuses, born in the water, grown in the water, remaining submerged in the water, nourished below the surface; some blue lotuses, red lotuses, or white lotuses, born in the water, grown in the water, standing level with the water; some blue lotuses, red lotuses, or white lotuses, born in the water, grown in the water, rising above the water, unadulterated by the water; in the same way, Bhikkhave, as I surveyed the world with the Buddha-eye, I saw beings with little dust in their eyes and with much dust in their eyes, with keen faculties and with dull faculties, with good dispositions and with bad dispositions, easy to teach and hard to teach, some who dwelt seeing fear in the blameworthy actions of the next world, and some who did not dwell seeing fear in the blameworthy actions of the next world. Then, Bhikkhave, I addressed Brahmā Sahampati with a verse—

974\. ‘Open are these doors to the deathless, \
let those with ears release their faith; \
with an apperception of trouble, I did not teach the Dhamma, \
the subtle Dhamma among humans, O Brahmā.’

975\. Then, Bhikkhave, Brahmā Sahampati, thinking, ‘I have indeed been given an opportunity by the Bhagavā to teach the Dhamma,’ bowed to me, circumambulated me keeping his right side towards me, and vanished right there.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Roman IAST)">
966\. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhikkhave, etadahosi—  ‘adhigato kho myāyaṃ dhammo gambhīro duddaso duranubodho santo paṇīto atakkāvacaro nipuṇo paṇḍitavedanīyo. Ālayarāmā kho panāyaṃ pajā ālayaratā ālayasammuditā. Ālayarāmāya kho pana pajāya ālayaratāya ālayasammuditāya duddasaṃ idaṃ ṭhānaṃ yadidaṃ—  idappaccayatā paṭiccasamuppādo. Idampi kho ṭhānaṃ duddasaṃ yadidaṃ—  sabbasaṅkhārasamatho sabbūpadhipaṭinissaggo taṇhākkhayo virāgo nirodho nibbānaṃ. Ahañceva kho pana dhammaṃ deseyyaṃ, pare ca me na ājāneyyuṃ, so mamassa kilamatho, sā mamassa vihesā’ti. Apissu maṃ, bhikkhave, imā anacchariyā gāthāyo paṭibhaṃsu pubbe assutapubbā—

967\. ‘Kicchena me adhigataṃ, \
halaṃ dāni pakāsituṃ; \
Rāgadosaparetehi, \
nāyaṃ dhammo susambudho. \
968\. Paṭisotagāmiṃ nipuṇaṃ, \
gambhīraṃ duddasaṃ aṇuṃ; \
Rāgarattā na dakkhanti, \
tamokhandhena āvuṭā’ti.

969\. Itiha me, bhikkhave, paṭisañcikkhato appossukkatāya cittaṃ namati, no dhammadesanāya. Atha kho, bhikkhave, brahmuno sahampatissa mama cetasā cetoparivitakkamaññāya etadahosi—  ‘nassati vata bho loko, vinassati vata bho loko, yatra hi nāma tathāgatassa arahato sammāsambuddhassa appossukkatāya cittaṃ namati, no dhammadesanāyā’ti. Atha kho, bhikkhave, brahmā sahampati—  seyyathāpi nāma balavā puriso samiñjitaṃ vā bāhaṃ pasāreyya, pasāritaṃ vā bāhaṃ samiñjeyya; evameva—  brahmaloke antarahito mama purato pāturahosi. Atha kho, bhikkhave, brahmā sahampati ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā yenāhaṃ tenañjaliṃ paṇāmetvā maṃ etadavoca—  ‘desetu, bhante, bhagavā dhammaṃ, desetu sugato dhammaṃ. Santi sattā apparajakkhajātikā, assavanatā dhammassa parihāyanti. Bhavissanti dhammassa aññātāro’ti. Idamavoca, bhikkhave, brahmā sahampati. Idaṃ vatvā athāparaṃ etadavoca—

970\. ‘Pāturahosi magadhesu pubbe, \
Dhammo asuddho samalehi cintito; \
Apāpuretaṃ amatassa dvāraṃ, \
Suṇantu dhammaṃ vimalenānubuddhaṃ. \
971\. Sele yathā pabbatamuddhaniṭṭhito, \
Yathāpi passe janataṃ samantato; \
Tathūpamaṃ dhammamayaṃ sumedha, \
Pāsādamāruyha samantacakkhu; \
Sokāvatiṇṇaṃ janatamapetasoko, \
Avekkhassu jātijarābhibhūtaṃ. \
972\. Uṭṭhehi vīra vijitasaṅgāma, \
Satthavāha aṇaṇa vicara loke; \
Desassu bhagavā dhammaṃ, \
Aññātāro bhavissantī’ti.

973\. Atha kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, brahmuno ca ajjhesanaṃ viditvā sattesu ca kāruññataṃ paṭicca buddhacakkhunā lokaṃ volokesiṃ. Addasaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, buddhacakkhunā lokaṃ volokento satte apparajakkhe mahārajakkhe, tikkhindriye mudindriye, svākāre dvākāre, suviññāpaye duviññāpaye, appekacce paralokavajjabhayadassāvine viharante, appekacce na paralokavajjabhayadassāvine viharante. Seyyathāpi nāma uppaliniyaṃ vā paduminiyaṃ vā puṇḍarīkiniyaṃ vā appekaccāni uppalāni vā padumāni vā puṇḍarīkāni vā udake jātāni udake saṃvaḍḍhāni udakānuggatāni antonimuggaposīni, appekaccāni uppalāni vā padumāni vā puṇḍarīkāni vā udake jātāni udake saṃvaḍḍhāni udakānuggatāni samodakaṃ ṭhitāni, appekaccāni uppalāni vā padumāni vā puṇḍarīkāni vā udake jātāni udake saṃvaḍḍhāni udakaṃ accuggamma ṭhitāni anupalittāni udakena; evameva kho ahaṃ, bhikkhave, buddhacakkhunā lokaṃ volokento addasaṃ satte apparajakkhe mahārajakkhe, tikkhindriye mudindriye, svākāre dvākāre, suviññāpaye duviññāpaye, appekacce paralokavajjabhayadassāvine viharante, appekacce na paralokavajjabhayadassāvine viharante. Atha khvāhaṃ, bhikkhave, brahmānaṃ sahampatiṃ gāthāya paccabhāsiṃ—

974\. ‘Apārutā tesaṃ amatassa dvārā, \
Ye sotavanto pamuñcantu saddhaṃ; \
Vihiṃsasaññī paguṇaṃ na bhāsiṃ, \
Dhammaṃ paṇītaṃ manujesu brahme’ti.

975\. Atha kho, bhikkhave, brahmā sahampati ‘katāvakāso khomhi bhagavatā dhammadesanāyā’ti maṃ abhivādetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā tatthevantaradhāyi.
</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Pāḷi (Brahmi)">
966\. 𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀬𑁆𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀅𑀥𑀺𑀕𑀢𑁄 𑀔𑁄 𑀫𑁆𑀬𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀕𑀫𑁆𑀪𑀻𑀭𑁄 𑀤𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑁄 𑀤𑀼𑀭𑀦𑀼𑀩𑁄𑀥𑁄 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀡𑀻𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀸𑀯𑀘𑀭𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀧𑀼𑀡𑁄 𑀧𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀺𑀢𑀯𑁂𑀤𑀦𑀻𑀬𑁄. 𑀆𑀮𑀬𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀸 𑀔𑁄 𑀧𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀧𑀚𑀸 𑀆𑀮𑀬𑀭𑀢𑀸 𑀆𑀮𑀬𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀸. 𑀆𑀮𑀬𑀭𑀸𑀫𑀸𑀬 𑀔𑁄 𑀧𑀦 𑀧𑀚𑀸𑀬 𑀆𑀮𑀬𑀭𑀢𑀸𑀬 𑀆𑀮𑀬𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀤𑀺𑀢𑀸𑀬 𑀤𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀁 𑀇𑀤𑀁 𑀞𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀬𑀤𑀺𑀤𑀁—  𑀇𑀤𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀬𑀢𑀸 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀲𑀫𑀼𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀸𑀤𑁄. 𑀇𑀤𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀺 𑀔𑁄 𑀞𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀤𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀁 𑀬𑀤𑀺𑀤𑀁—  𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀔𑀸𑀭𑀲𑀫𑀣𑁄 𑀲𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀽𑀧𑀥𑀺𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀦𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀕𑁆𑀕𑁄 𑀢𑀡𑁆𑀳𑀸𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀬𑁄 𑀯𑀺𑀭𑀸𑀕𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀭𑁄𑀥𑁄 𑀦𑀺𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸𑀦𑀁. 𑀅𑀳𑀜𑁆𑀘𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑁄 𑀧𑀦 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀤𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀁, 𑀧𑀭𑁂 𑀘 𑀫𑁂 𑀦 𑀆𑀚𑀸𑀦𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀼𑀁, 𑀲𑁄 𑀫𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀓𑀺𑀮𑀫𑀣𑁄, 𑀲𑀸 𑀫𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑁂𑀲𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀧𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀫𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀇𑀫𑀸 𑀅𑀦𑀘𑁆𑀙𑀭𑀺𑀬𑀸 𑀕𑀸𑀣𑀸𑀬𑁄 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀪𑀁𑀲𑀼 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁂 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀢𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀸—

967\. ‘𑀓𑀺𑀘𑁆𑀙𑁂𑀦 𑀫𑁂 𑀅𑀥𑀺𑀕𑀢𑀁, \
𑀳𑀮𑀁 𑀤𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀧𑀓𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀢𑀼𑀁; \
𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀤𑁄𑀲𑀧𑀭𑁂𑀢𑁂𑀳𑀺, \
𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀲𑀼𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀼𑀥𑁄. \
968\. 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀲𑁄𑀢𑀕𑀸𑀫𑀺𑀁 𑀦𑀺𑀧𑀼𑀡𑀁, \
𑀕𑀫𑁆𑀪𑀻𑀭𑀁 𑀤𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀁 𑀅𑀡𑀼𑀁; \
𑀭𑀸𑀕𑀭𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀦 𑀤𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺, \
𑀢𑀫𑁄𑀔𑀦𑁆𑀥𑁂𑀦 𑀆𑀯𑀼𑀝𑀸’𑀢𑀺.

969\. 𑀇𑀢𑀺𑀳 𑀫𑁂, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁄𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀢𑀸𑀬 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦𑀫𑀢𑀺, 𑀦𑁄 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸𑀬. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀦𑁄 𑀲𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀫𑀫 𑀘𑁂𑀢𑀲𑀸 𑀘𑁂𑀢𑁄𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀯𑀺𑀢𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀫𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀬 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺—  ‘𑀦𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺 𑀯𑀢 𑀪𑁄 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁄, 𑀯𑀺𑀦𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀢𑀺 𑀯𑀢 𑀪𑁄 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁄, 𑀬𑀢𑁆𑀭 𑀳𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀢𑀣𑀸𑀕𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀭𑀳𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀸𑀲𑀫𑁆𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁄𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼𑀓𑁆𑀓𑀢𑀸𑀬 𑀘𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀁 𑀦𑀫𑀢𑀺, 𑀦𑁄 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀲𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑀺—  𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀩𑀮𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀺𑀲𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀧𑀲𑀸𑀭𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬, 𑀧𑀲𑀸𑀭𑀺𑀢𑀁 𑀯𑀸 𑀩𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀚𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬; 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑁂𑀯—  𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁂 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀳𑀺𑀢𑁄 𑀫𑀫 𑀧𑀼𑀭𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀸𑀢𑀼𑀭𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀲𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑀺 𑀏𑀓𑀁𑀲𑀁 𑀉𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀸𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀕𑀁 𑀓𑀭𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀬𑁂𑀦𑀸𑀳𑀁 𑀢𑁂𑀦𑀜𑁆𑀚𑀮𑀺𑀁 𑀧𑀡𑀸𑀫𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀫𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—  ‘𑀤𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀢𑀼, 𑀪𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, 𑀤𑁂𑀲𑁂𑀢𑀼 𑀲𑀼𑀕𑀢𑁄 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁. 𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀭𑀚𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀓𑀸, 𑀅𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀯𑀦𑀢𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀧𑀭𑀺𑀳𑀸𑀬𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺. 𑀪𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀅𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀭𑁄’𑀢𑀺. 𑀇𑀤𑀫𑀯𑁄𑀘, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀲𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑀺. 𑀇𑀤𑀁 𑀯𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀭𑀁 𑀏𑀢𑀤𑀯𑁄𑀘—

970\. ‘𑀧𑀸𑀢𑀼𑀭𑀳𑁄𑀲𑀺 𑀫𑀕𑀥𑁂𑀲𑀼 𑀧𑀼𑀩𑁆𑀩𑁂, \
𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑁄 𑀅𑀲𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑁄 𑀲𑀫𑀮𑁂𑀳𑀺 𑀘𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀺𑀢𑁄; \
𑀅𑀧𑀸𑀧𑀼𑀭𑁂𑀢𑀁 𑀅𑀫𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀭𑀁, \
𑀲𑀼𑀡𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀼 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀫𑀮𑁂𑀦𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀁. \
971\. 𑀲𑁂𑀮𑁂 𑀬𑀣𑀸 𑀧𑀩𑁆𑀩𑀢𑀫𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀦𑀺𑀝𑁆𑀞𑀺𑀢𑁄, \
𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀧𑀲𑁆𑀲𑁂 𑀚𑀦𑀢𑀁 𑀲𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀢𑁄; \
𑀢𑀣𑀽𑀧𑀫𑀁 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀫𑀬𑀁 𑀲𑀼𑀫𑁂𑀥, \
𑀧𑀸𑀲𑀸𑀤𑀫𑀸𑀭𑀼𑀬𑁆𑀳 𑀲𑀫𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼; \
𑀲𑁄𑀓𑀸𑀯𑀢𑀺𑀡𑁆𑀡𑀁 𑀚𑀦𑀢𑀫𑀧𑁂𑀢𑀲𑁄𑀓𑁄, \
𑀅𑀯𑁂𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀺𑀚𑀭𑀸𑀪𑀺𑀪𑀽𑀢𑀁. \
972\. 𑀉𑀝𑁆𑀞𑁂𑀳𑀺 𑀯𑀻𑀭 𑀯𑀺𑀚𑀺𑀢𑀲𑀗𑁆𑀕𑀸𑀫, \
𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀣𑀯𑀸𑀳 𑀅𑀡𑀡 𑀯𑀺𑀘𑀭 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁂; \
𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀼 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁, \
𑀅𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀭𑁄 𑀪𑀯𑀺𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀻’𑀢𑀺.

973\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀼𑀦𑁄 𑀘 𑀅𑀚𑁆𑀛𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀁 𑀯𑀺𑀤𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂𑀲𑀼 𑀘 𑀓𑀸𑀭𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀢𑀁 𑀧𑀝𑀺𑀘𑁆𑀘 𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼𑀦𑀸 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀁 𑀯𑁄𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁂𑀲𑀺𑀁. 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀁 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼𑀦𑀸 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀁 𑀯𑁄𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀭𑀚𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂 𑀫𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀚𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂, 𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁆𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂 𑀫𑀼𑀤𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁆𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂, 𑀲𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀭𑁂 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀭𑁂, 𑀲𑀼𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀧𑀬𑁂 𑀤𑀼𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀧𑀬𑁂, 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁂 𑀧𑀭𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀯𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀪𑀬𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁂 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁂 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀯𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀪𑀬𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁂 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂. 𑀲𑁂𑀬𑁆𑀬𑀣𑀸𑀧𑀺 𑀦𑀸𑀫 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀮𑀺𑀦𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀤𑀼𑀫𑀺𑀦𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀭𑀻𑀓𑀺𑀦𑀺𑀬𑀁 𑀯𑀸 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀮𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀤𑀼𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀭𑀻𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂 𑀲𑀁𑀯𑀟𑁆𑀠𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀅𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄𑀦𑀺𑀫𑀼𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀧𑁄𑀲𑀻𑀦𑀺, 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀮𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀤𑀼𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀭𑀻𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂 𑀲𑀁𑀯𑀟𑁆𑀠𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀼𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀲𑀫𑁄𑀤𑀓𑀁 𑀞𑀺𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺, 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀮𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀤𑀼𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀼𑀡𑁆𑀟𑀭𑀻𑀓𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀯𑀸 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂 𑀚𑀸𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂 𑀲𑀁𑀯𑀟𑁆𑀠𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑀁 𑀅𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀼𑀕𑁆𑀕𑀫𑁆𑀫 𑀞𑀺𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀅𑀦𑀼𑀧𑀮𑀺𑀢𑁆𑀢𑀸𑀦𑀺 𑀉𑀤𑀓𑁂𑀦; 𑀏𑀯𑀫𑁂𑀯 𑀔𑁄 𑀅𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑀼𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀘𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀼𑀦𑀸 𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀁 𑀯𑁄𑀮𑁄𑀓𑁂𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀅𑀤𑁆𑀤𑀲𑀁 𑀲𑀢𑁆𑀢𑁂 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑀭𑀚𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂 𑀫𑀳𑀸𑀭𑀚𑀓𑁆𑀔𑁂, 𑀢𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁆𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂 𑀫𑀼𑀤𑀺𑀦𑁆𑀤𑁆𑀭𑀺𑀬𑁂, 𑀲𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀭𑁂 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀓𑀸𑀭𑁂, 𑀲𑀼𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀧𑀬𑁂 𑀤𑀼𑀯𑀺𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀸𑀧𑀬𑁂, 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁂 𑀧𑀭𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀯𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀪𑀬𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁂 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂, 𑀅𑀧𑁆𑀧𑁂𑀓𑀘𑁆𑀘𑁂 𑀦 𑀧𑀭𑀮𑁄𑀓𑀯𑀚𑁆𑀚𑀪𑀬𑀤𑀲𑁆𑀲𑀸𑀯𑀺𑀦𑁂 𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀭𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁂. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀳𑀁, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀸𑀦𑀁 𑀲𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑀺𑀁 𑀕𑀸𑀣𑀸𑀬 𑀧𑀘𑁆𑀘𑀪𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁—

974\. ‘𑀅𑀧𑀸𑀭𑀼𑀢𑀸 𑀢𑁂𑀲𑀁 𑀅𑀫𑀢𑀲𑁆𑀲 𑀤𑁆𑀯𑀸𑀭𑀸, \
𑀬𑁂 𑀲𑁄𑀢𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑁄 𑀧𑀫𑀼𑀜𑁆𑀘𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀼 𑀲𑀤𑁆𑀥𑀁; \
𑀯𑀺𑀳𑀺𑀁𑀲𑀲𑀜𑁆𑀜𑀻 𑀧𑀕𑀼𑀡𑀁 𑀦 𑀪𑀸𑀲𑀺𑀁, \
𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀁 𑀧𑀡𑀻𑀢𑀁 𑀫𑀦𑀼𑀚𑁂𑀲𑀼 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑁂’𑀢𑀺.

975\. 𑀅𑀣 𑀔𑁄, 𑀪𑀺𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀯𑁂, 𑀩𑁆𑀭𑀳𑁆𑀫𑀸 𑀲𑀳𑀫𑁆𑀧𑀢𑀺 ‘𑀓𑀢𑀸𑀯𑀓𑀸𑀲𑁄 𑀔𑁄𑀫𑁆𑀳𑀺 𑀪𑀕𑀯𑀢𑀸 𑀥𑀫𑁆𑀫𑀤𑁂𑀲𑀦𑀸𑀬𑀸’𑀢𑀺 𑀫𑀁 𑀅𑀪𑀺𑀯𑀸𑀤𑁂𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀧𑀤𑀓𑁆𑀔𑀺𑀡𑀁 𑀓𑀢𑁆𑀯𑀸 𑀢𑀢𑁆𑀣𑁂𑀯𑀦𑁆𑀢𑀭𑀥𑀸𑀬𑀺.
</TabItem>
